containing the delta form on
N\times N
. A version of the axial gauge for Chern–Simons theory was first employed in
[
21
]
. In our situation,
N=\Sigma
and
M=I
is not a closed manifold and hence the construction has to be adapted for boundary conditions — which is exactly what we did above. The chain contraction, corresponding to (
21
), has the form
K=K_{I,\partial I}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}^{+}}+K_{I}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}^{-}}
. We will encounter versions of this construction for different choices of boundary conditions
further in this paper (e.g., in the case of Section
4.2
, the chain contraction has the form
K_{I,\{1\}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}^{+}}+K_{I,\{0\}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}^{-}}
).
20
20

20

Depending on the choice of boundary conditions (e.g., in the case of Section
4.2
), the space of forms subject to boundary conditions
\mathcal{Y}[-1]
may fail to be a subcomplex of
\Omega^{\bullet}(I\times\Sigma)
with respect to the total de Rham differential
d_{I}+d_{\Sigma}
. However, the operator
K
we are constructing can be seen as a chain contraction for just the “axial” differential
d_{I}
.

The BV-BFV partition function is readily calculated:

(22)

Z(\underbrace{\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}}}_{\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}},\underbrace{\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}},\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}}_{\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}};\underbrace{\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}},\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}}_{\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}},\underbrace{\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}},\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}}_{\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}})=\\
=\int_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{K-ex}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S^{f}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\widetilde{\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}+dt\cdot\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\right)}\\
=\int\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}\big(\int_{I\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}d_{I}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\int_{\{1\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{-}-\int_{\{0\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}\mathsf{A}^{-}+\int_{I\times\Sigma}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}\,d_{\Sigma}\mathcal{A}\big)}\\
=\int\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\Big(\int_{I\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\,d_{I}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\big|_{t=1})-\\
-\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\big|_{t=0})+\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\int_{I\times\Sigma}dt\;\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\Big).

Here we are using the splitting
d_{\Sigma}=\partial+\bar{\partial}
of de Rham operator on
\Sigma
into the holomorphic and antiholomorphic Dolbeault operators. Finally, computing this Gaussian integral, we obtain

(23)

Z=\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\int_{\Sigma}\Big((\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}})\;\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}})\;\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\Big).

Here the last term arises from the
Wick contraction

\big\langle\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\hbox to11.91pt{\vbox to8.64pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.95488pt\lower-4.3214pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

and a similar one with
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}
talking to
\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}
.

Graphically, the diagrams contributing to (
23
) are:

Figure 1
.
Feynman diagrams for the abelian theory on a cylinder in holomorphic-to-holomorphic polarization.

Here the conventions (Feynman rules) are:

•

Black dots are vertices, which can be on in- or out-boundary (then they are univalent, with single incoming half-edge), or in the bulk (then they are bivalent – with one incoming and one outgoing, or with two outgoing half-edges).

•

Half-edges can be internal (joined into pairs forming an edge, depicted as a long edge above) or external – depicted as a short edge ending with a white or a gray blob, depending on orientation.

•

Boundary vertices are decorated by
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}
on the out-boundary and by
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}
on the in-boundary.

•

White blobs are decorated by
\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}
, gray blobs are decorated by
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}
.

•

(Long) edges are decorated by the propagator
\eta
.

•

Bulk vertices with one incoming and one outgoing half-edge carry
\partial
; bulk vertices with two outgoing half-edges carry
\bar{\partial}
.
(Equivalently, one may say that bulk vertices are decorated by
d_{\Sigma}
independently of orientation.)

•

For each connected graph
\Gamma
in Figure
1
, we take the product of decorations obtaining a differential form on
\mathrm{Conf}_{\Gamma}=\Sigma^{\#\mathrm{in-vertices}}\times(I\times\Sigma)^{\#\mathrm{bulk\,vertices}}\times\Sigma^{\#\mathrm{out-vertices}}
depending on the residual fields. Then we take integral over
\mathrm{Conf}_{\Gamma}
, obtaining the value of the diagram.

We will return to the version of the result (
23
) in the context of
nonabelian
Chern–Simons theory in Section
5.4
.

4.1.1.
Comparison with Hamilton–Jacobi action

We can write the result (
23
) in the form

(24)

Z=\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\Sigma}\Big((\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}})\lambda+\lambda\partial\sigma+\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}})\bar{\partial}\sigma\Big)}_{S_{\text{HJ}}}+\\
+\int_{\Sigma}\Big((\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}})\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}})\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\Big)\Big),

where we introduced the alternative notation for degree zero residual fields

\lambda\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}},\;\;\sigma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}.

In the first integral in (
24
) we recognize the Hamilton–Jacobi action
[
16
, Eq. (48)]
, which can be seen as the conformal
\beta\gamma
-system coupled to the boundary fields, while in the second integral we collected the contribution of nonzero-degree fields.

4.1.2.
Quantum master equation

The space of states on a surface with
\mathsf{A}^{+}
-fixed polarization is the space of functions of
\mathsf{A}^{+}
of the form

(25)

\Psi(\mathsf{A}^{+})=\sum_{n\geq 0}\int_{\Sigma^{n}}\gamma_{n}\pi_{1}^{*}\mathsf{A}^{+}\cdots\pi_{n}^{*}\mathsf{A}^{+}

where
\{\gamma_{n}\}
are
\hbar
-dependent
distributional forms on
\Sigma^{n}
and
\pi_{i}\colon\Sigma^{n}\rightarrow\Sigma
is the projection to the
i
-th copy of
\Sigma
(we refer to
[
15
, Section 3.5.1]
for details).
The space of states is
equipped with the differential (the quantum BFV operator)

(26)

\Omega_{\Sigma}^{+}=\int_{\Sigma}\left(-i\hbar\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}\,\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}}+\epsilon\mathsf{A}^{1,0}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}\right)

with the sign
\epsilon=+1
for the out-boundary and
\epsilon=-1
for the in-boundary;
21
21

21

The integral over
\Sigma
in (
26
) is understood as being with respect to the “standard” orientation, which coincides with the induced one from the cylinder on the out-boundary and is opposite to the induced one on the in-boundary.

the superscript in
\Omega^{+}_{\Sigma}
is a reminder of the choice of polarization. This operator is the canonical quantization of the boundary BFV action (
19
),

(27)

S_{\Sigma}=\epsilon\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}+\mathsf{A}^{0,1}\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}.

In the quantization,
\mathsf{A}^{0},\mathsf{A}^{1,0}
become multiplication operators and
\mathsf{A}^{0,1}\mapsto-\epsilon\,i\hbar\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}}
,
\mathsf{A}^{2}\mapsto-\epsilon\,i\hbar\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0}}
become derivations.

Lemma 4.2
.

The partition function (
23
) satisfies the BV quantum master equation modified by the boundary terms (see
[
15
]
):

(28)

\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\Sigma}\big(-i\hbar\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\big)}_{\Omega_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}}+\underbrace{\int_{\Sigma}\big(-i\hbar\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}}}-\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}}\big)}_{\Omega_{\mathrm{in}}^{+}}-\\
-\hbar^{2}\underbrace{\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}}\;\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}}}_{\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}}\Big)\;Z=0.

Proof.

One checks this by a direct computation:

(29)

(\Omega_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}+\Omega_{\mathrm{in}}^{+})Z=\\
=Z\cdot\int_{\Sigma}\Big((\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}})\,\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}(\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}})\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}}\Big).

On the other hand,

(30)

\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}Z=Z\cdot\int_{\Sigma}\big(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}-\partial\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\big)\,\big(\partial\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{in}})\big).

Inspecting this expression, we see that it coincides with (
29
), which proves (
28
).
∎

Following the terminology of
[
15
]
, we call the equation
(\Omega_{\partial}-\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{res}})Z=0
the
modified
(by the boundary term) quantum master equation (mQME).

4.2.
Antiholomorphic-to-holomorphic boundary conditions

Consider the polarization
\mathrm{Span}\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}}\}
at
t=0
and
\mathrm{Span}\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}}\}
at
t=1
. I.e., we prescribe boundary values
\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}
. The corresponding modification of the action by boundary terms adjusting for the polarization is:

S^{f}=S+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{1\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}\mathsf{A}^{-}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{0\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}\mathsf{A}^{-}

and the modified boundary Noether
1
-form is:

\alpha^{f}_{\Sigma\times\partial I}=\int_{\{1\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}-\int_{\{0\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}.

The fiber of the (complexified) space of fields over the space of boundary conditions
\mathcal{B}=\mathfrak{g}^{-}[1]\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{+}[1]=\{(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}})\}
is the complex

\mathcal{Y}=\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{1\};\mathfrak{g}^{+}[1])\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{0\};\mathfrak{g}^{-}[1]).

Hodge decomposition (
14
) holds (where one replaces
\Pi\rightarrow[1]
) and the propagator is given by (
15
) or, more explicitly,

\eta((z,\bar{z},t)\,;\,(z^{\prime},\bar{z}^{\prime},t^{\prime}))=\\
=\delta^{(2)}(z-z^{\prime})\frac{i}{2}(-dz\wedge d\bar{z}^{\prime}+dz^{\prime}\wedge d\bar{z}^{\prime})\;\big(-\theta(t^{\prime}-t)+2(1-t)\,t^{\prime}\big)+\\
+\delta^{(2)}(z-z^{\prime})\frac{i}{2}(d\bar{z}\wedge dz^{\prime}+dz\wedge d\bar{z})\;\big(\theta(t-t^{\prime})-2t\,(1-t^{\prime})\big).

The space of residual fields is:

(31)

\mathcal{V}=dt\cdot\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus(1-t)\cdot\mathfrak{g}^{+}[1]\oplus t\cdot\mathfrak{g}^{-}[1]=\{dt\cdot\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}+(1-t)\cdot\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+t\cdot\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}\},

where
\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}
,
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}
,
\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}
are
t
-independent forms on
\Sigma
. The homogeneous components of these residual fields
and their internal degrees (ghost numbers) are as follows:

\mathsf{A}_{{I\,\mathrm{res}}}=

\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}^{0}+

\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}^{1,0}+

\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}^{0,1}+

\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}^{2}

\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}=

\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}+

\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}}

\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}=

\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+

\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}

0

-1

-1

-2

1

0

0

-1

The BV-BFV partition function is:

(32)

Z(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}};\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}},\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}},\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}})=\\
=\int_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{K-ex}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S^{f}\Big(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+(1-t)\cdot\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+t\cdot\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}+dt\cdot\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\Big)}\\
=\int\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}\Big(\int_{I\times\Sigma}\big(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}+t\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}\big)\,d_{I}\big(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+(1-t)\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}\big)+\int_{\{1\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{-}-\int_{\{0\}\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}\mathsf{A}^{+}+\int_{I\times\Sigma}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}\,d_{\Sigma}\mathcal{A}\Big)}\\
=\int\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\;\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\Big(\int_{I\times\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\,d_{I}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}(\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}\big|_{t=1})-\\
-\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\big|_{t=0})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\;d_{\Sigma}(\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}})\Big)\\
=\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\int_{\Sigma}\Big(-\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}-\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}+\\
+\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}(\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}})\big)\Big).

Here the first term in the final result is a contribution of the diagram where
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}
is contracted by a propagator with
\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}
.

4.2.1.
Partial integral over residual fields and comparison with Hamilton–Jacobi action

Motivated by comparison with the Hamilton–Jacobi formalism, we consider the BV pushforward of the partition function (
32
) along the odd symplectic fibration

p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{small}}=\{dt\cdot(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}+\mathsf{A}^{2}_{I\,\mathrm{res}})+(1-t)\cdot\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}+t\cdot\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}\}.

In its kernel, we choose the gauge-fixing lagrangian subspace
\mathcal{L}
cut out by equations
\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}=\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}=0
and parametrized by
\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}},\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}
. The corresponding BV pushforward is:

(33)

Z_{\mathrm{small}}=\int\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}}\,\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}\;Z=\\
=\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}+(\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}+\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}})\;\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\sigma\partial\bar{\partial}\sigma\Big)}_{S_{\text{HJ}}}+\\
+\int_{\Sigma}\Big(-\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}+\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}-\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}\Big)\Big).

Here we denoted the degree zero scalar residual field by

\sigma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}\;\;\in\Omega^{0}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Sigma).

In the first bracket in (
33
) we recognize the Hamilton–Jacobi action
[
16
, Eq. (47)]
(see also Example
2.2
) --- the action of a free (conformal) massless boson interacting with the boundary fields,
22
22

22

One can see
S_{\text{HJ}}
as the abelian version of the gauged Wess–Zumino–Witten theory, see e.g (2.7) in
[
24
]
.

while in the second bracket we collected the contributions of nonzero-degree fields.

4.2.2.
Full integral over residual fields

If we wish to integrate out the remaining residual fields completely, we construct the gauge-fixing lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{small}}\subset\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{small}}
as follows. Choose an area form
\mu
on
\Sigma
. Consider the splitting of
0
-forms into constants and forms with vanishing integral against
\mu
:
\mathsf{A}^{0}=\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{c}}+\underline{\mathsf{A}^{0}}
.
Also, consider the splitting of
2
-forms into constant multiples of
\mu
and forms of vanishing total integral:
\mathsf{A}^{2}=\mu\cdot\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{c}}+\underline{\mathsf{A}^{2}}
. Then, we define the lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{small}}\subset\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{small}}
by equations
\mathsf{A}^{2}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}=\sigma_{\mathrm{c}}=\underline{\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}}=0
. Thus, the lagrangian is parametrized by
\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}
,
\underline{\sigma}
,
\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res},\mathrm{c}}
.
23
23

23

We have to split off the constants from
\sigma
, because they are in the kernel of the Laplacian
\partial\bar{\partial}
and thus would obstruct the evaluation of the integral of (
33
) over
\sigma
.

The resulting full BV integral is:

Z_{*}=\int_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{small}}\subset\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{small}}}\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}\;\mathcal{D}\underline{\sigma}\;\mathcal{D}\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res},\mathrm{c}}\;\;Z_{\mathrm{small}}=\\
=\delta(\underline{\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}})e^{-\frac{i}{\hbar}\int_{\Sigma}\mu\cdot\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{c}}}\int\mathcal{D}\underline{\sigma}\;\exp\frac{i}{\hbar}\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}+(\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}+\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}})\;\underline{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\sigma}\partial\bar{\partial}\underline{\sigma}\Big).

Further, assume that the area form
\mu=\sqrt{\det{g}}\;d^{2}x
is the Riemannian area form associated to a certain metric
g
on
\Sigma
inducing simultaneously the complex structure we use in our polarization. Then the integral over
\underline{\sigma}
evaluates finally to

(34)

Z_{*}=\delta(\underline{\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}})\;e^{-\frac{i}{\hbar}\int_{\Sigma}\mu\cdot\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{c}}}\;\cdot\big({\det}^{\prime}_{\Omega^{0}(\Sigma)}\Delta_{g}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}\mathbb{I}(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}},\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}})}

where

•

\Delta_{g}
is the metric Laplace operator acting on
0
-forms,
{\det}^{\prime}
means the zeta-regularized product of
nonzero
eigenvalues.

•

The exponent in (
34
) is

(35)

\mathbb{I}=\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}P_{\mathrm{harm}}(\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}})-i\int_{\Sigma\times\Sigma\;\ni(z,z^{\prime})}\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\big|_{z}G(z,z^{\prime})\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\big|_{z^{\prime}}+\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}\big|_{z}G(z,z^{\prime})\,\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}\big|_{z^{\prime}}.

Here
G
is the Green’s function for
\Delta_{g}
, viewed as a function on
\Sigma\times\Sigma
with a logarithmic singularity at the diagonal.
24
24

24

Recall that, in terms of Dolbeault operators and the area form, the metric Laplace operator is:
\Delta_{g}=\frac{2i}{\mu}\partial\bar{\partial}
.

The operator
P_{\mathrm{harm}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}\mapsto\mathsf{A}^{0,1}-2i\int_{\Sigma\ni z^{\prime}}\bar{\partial}G(z,z^{\prime})\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}\big|_{z^{\prime}}
is the projector onto harmonic
(0,1)
-forms in the Hodge decomposition.

Written in different notations, the exponent in (
34
) is:

(36)

\mathbb{I}=\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,(1-\bar{\partial}(\partial\bar{\partial})^{-1}\partial)\,\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\bar{\partial}(\partial\bar{\partial})^{-1}\bar{\partial}\,\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\partial(\partial\bar{\partial})^{-1}\partial\,\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}.

Remark 4.3
.

(a)

The exponent
\mathbb{I}
in (
34
) depends only on the complex structure on
\Sigma
, not on the particular compatible metric
g
. In other words, it is invariant under Weyl transformations of the metric
g\mapsto e^{\phi}\,g
. Weyl-invariance of
\mathbb{I}
is manifest in the form (
36
).

(b)

Unlike
\mathbb{I}
, the full quantum answer (
34
) is not Weyl-invariant, since the determinant of the Laplacian is not invariant (a phenomenon known as the “conformal anomaly” or “trace anomaly” of the free scalar field as a conformal field theory).
In addition to that quantum effect, the dependence of
Z_{*}
on boundary
\mathrm{gh}\neq 0
fields
\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}^{2},\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}
involves the metric area form
\mu
.

(c)

The lagrangian generated by
\mathbb{I}
is

L_{\mathbb{I}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}=\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}^{1,0}+\frac{\delta\mathbb{I}}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}},\\
\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}=\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}^{0,1}-\frac{\delta\mathbb{I}}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}}\end{array}\right\}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}=(1-\bar{\partial}(\partial\bar{\partial})^{-1}\bar{\partial})\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}+P_{\mathrm{harm}}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}},\\
\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}=(1+\partial(\partial\bar{\partial})^{-1}\partial)\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}+P_{\mathrm{harm}}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\end{array}\right\}.

It is easy to see that
this lagrangian coincides with
the evolution relation of abelian Chern–Simons theory on the cylinder
I\times\Sigma
,

L_{CS}=\big\{(\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}},\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}})\;\in\Omega^{1}(\Sigma)\times\Omega^{1}(\Sigma)\;\;\big|\;\;d\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}=0,\;d\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}=0,\;\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in}}=d(\cdots)\big\}.

Thus,
\mathbb{I}
is a (nongeneralized
25
25

25

I.e., with no auxiliary fields.
) Hamilton–Jacobi action for the abelian theory on the cylinder.

(d)

Classically, one can obtain
\mathbb{I}
from the generalized Hamilton–Jacobi action (Example
2.2
)
as the conditional extremum of
S_{\text{HJ}}
in
\sigma
, with
\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}
and
\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}
fixed.

Remark 4.4
.

To make (
b
) of Remark
4.3
above more explicit: if
g_{\tau}=e^{\phi_{\tau}}g_{0}
is a
\tau
-dependent family of metrics compatible with the given complex structure on
\Sigma
, one has

(37)

\frac{d}{d\tau}Z_{*}^{g_{\tau}}=(\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}})(\xi Z_{*}^{g_{\tau}})+Z_{*}^{g_{\tau}}\cdot\frac{1}{48\pi}\int_{\Sigma}\mu_{g_{\tau}}R_{g_{\tau}}\dot{\phi}_{\tau}

with
R
the scalar curvature of the metric,
\mu_{g_{\tau}}
the Riemannian area form of
g_{\tau}
, the
\Omega
operators given by (
40
), (
41
) below and
26
26

26

This is the quantization of the hamiltonian
H=\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res},c}\int_{\Sigma}\underline{\sigma}\dot{\mu}
generating the family of lagrangians given by
\mu_{g^{\tau}}
, see
[
15
, Section 2]
.

\xi=\mathsf{A}_{\mathrm{in},c}\int_{\Sigma\times\Sigma}(\bar{\partial}A^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\partial A^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}})_{z}G(z,z^{\prime})\dot{\mu}_{z^{\prime}}
. The second term in (
37
) is the trace anomaly.
Furthermore, one can compensate the anomaly term by including the Liouville action as a counterterm,
27
27

27

We are referring to the fact that in a conformal field theory with central charge
c
, the partition function has the following behavior under Weyl transformations of metric:

Z_{\mathrm{CFT}}^{e^{\phi}g}=Z_{\mathrm{CFT}}^{g}\cdot e^{\frac{c}{48\pi}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{1}{2}d\phi\wedge*d\phi+R\phi\mu_{g}}
, see, e.g.,
[
22
]
.

i.e., by introducing

\widehat{Z}^{g}=Z_{*}^{g}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{48\pi}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{1}{2}d\phi\wedge*d\phi+R_{g}\phi\mu_{g}},

where
\phi
is defined by
g=e^{\phi}g_{0}
with
g_{0}
some “reference” metric in the same conformal class (e.g., one can take
g_{0}
to be the uniformization metric on
\Sigma
—
spherical, flat or hyperbolic metric for
\Sigma
of genus
0
,
1
or
\geq 2
, respectively). Then, for a conformal variation of metric we have
\delta_{\varphi}\widehat{Z}^{e^{\varphi}g}=(\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}})(\xi Z_{*}^{g})
.

As an aside, it is tempting to compare the two phenomena:

(i)

The anomalous metric dependence (under a Weyl transformation
g_{\Sigma}\rightarrow e^{\phi}g_{\Sigma}
) of the partition function on the cylinder and the cancellation of that dependence by a Liouville action counterterm.

(ii)

The anomalous metric dependence (under
g_{M}\rightarrow g_{M}+\delta g_{M}
) of the perturbative Chern–Simons partition function on a closed 3-manifold
M
and the cancellation of that dependence by the gravitational Chern–Simons counterterm introducing the dependence on framing
M
, see
[
41
,
5
]
.

But in fact, these effects seem different. In particular, the dependence on Weyl transformations in (
i
) rescales
Z
by a real factor, whereas the anomalous metric dependence in (
ii
) affects only the phase of the partition function.

Remark 4.5
.

As implied by (
33
),
one can view the “physical part”
Z_{*}^{\mathrm{ph}}=e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}\mathbb{I}(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}},\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}})}
of (
34
) as a generating function for the correlators of chiral currents
j=i\partial\phi
,
\bar{j}=i\bar{\partial}\phi
in massless scalar theory (viewed as abelian WZW model):

(38)

\langle j(z_{1})\cdots j(z_{n})\bar{j}(w_{1})\cdots\bar{j}(w_{m})\rangle=\\
=\hbar^{n+m}\frac{1}{Z^{\mathrm{ph}}_{*}}\left.\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}(z_{1})}\cdots\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}(z_{n})}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}(w_{1})}\cdots\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}(w_{m})}\right|_{\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}=\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}=0}Z^{\mathrm{ph}}_{*}

Here we are assuming that all points
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n},w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}
in
\Sigma
are pairwise distinct (so that we can ignore the term
\int_{\Sigma}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}
in (
33
)).
We note that (
36
) implies a short-distance behavior of these correlators consistent with the OPEs (operator product expansions)

(39)

j(z)j(w)\sim\frac{1}{(z-w)^{2}}+\mathrm{reg},\quad\bar{j}(z)\bar{j}(w)\sim\frac{1}{(\bar{z}-\bar{w})^{2}}+\mathrm{reg},\quad j(z)\bar{j}(w)\sim\mathrm{reg}

as
z\rightarrow w
(“reg” stands for the regular part) – the standard fundamental OPEs of abelian WZW model.

4.2.3.
Quantum master equation

The space of states on the out-surface with
\mathsf{A}^{+}
-fixed polarization was discussed in Section
4.1.2
: it is the space of functions of
\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}
of the form (
25
) with the BFV operator

(40)

\Omega_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}=\int_{\Sigma}\left(-i\hbar\;\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\right).

The space of states on the in-surface with
\mathsf{A}^{-}
-fixed polarization is the space of functions of
\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}
(defined similarly to (
25
)) with the BFV operator

(41)

\Omega_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}=\int_{\Sigma}\Big(-i\hbar\;\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\hbar^{2}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}}\,\bar{\partial}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{in}}}\Big).

This is the quantization of the BFV action (
27
) where
\mathsf{A}^{0,1},\mathsf{A}^{2}
become multiplication operators and
\mathsf{A}^{1,0}\mapsto-\epsilon\,i\hbar\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0,1}}
,
\mathsf{A}^{0}\mapsto-\epsilon\,i\hbar\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{2}}
become derivations, where
\epsilon=-1
for the in-boundary, as in Section
4.1.2
.

The BV Laplacian on residual fields (
31
) is:
28
28

28

Here the factor
2
comes from the fact that the odd symplectic form on
\mathcal{V}
, induced from the standard BV 2-form on the space of fields,
\omega_{\mathcal{F}}=\int_{I\times\Sigma}\frac{1}{2}\delta\mathcal{A}\,\delta\mathcal{A}
, is
\omega_{\mathcal{V}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}\,\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}+\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}\,\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}
. The factor
\frac{1}{2}
in the latter expression comes from
\int_{0}^{1}dt(1-t)=\int_{0}^{1}dt\,t=\frac{1}{2}
.

\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}=2\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}_{\mathrm{res}}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}}+\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{+}_{\mathrm{res}}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{-}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}}.

Lemma 4.6
.

Partition function (
32
) satisfies the modified quantum master equation

\left(\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}-\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}\right)\;Z=0.

Proof.

Indeed, a straightforward computation gives:

\displaystyle\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}Z

\displaystyle=Z\cdot\int_{\Sigma}\Big(-\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}-\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}})+\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}\;\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}\Big),

\displaystyle\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}Z

\displaystyle=Z\cdot\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}})-(\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}})\;\bar{\partial}(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}-\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}})\Big),

\displaystyle-\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}Z

\displaystyle=Z\cdot\int_{\Sigma}\Big((\partial\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}})\,(-\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}})+

\displaystyle+\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}\,

\displaystyle(-\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{out}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}})+\partial\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{res}}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}\mathsf{A}^{0}_{I\,\mathrm{res}})\Big).

The sum of these three expressions is zero.
∎

Similarly, one can check the quantum master equation for the “small” realization (
33
):

\left(\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}-\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{small}}\right)\;Z_{\mathrm{small}}=0,

where

\Delta_{\mathrm{small}}=2\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{2}_{\mathrm{res}}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\sigma}+\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{res}}}\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathsf{A}^{2}_{I\,\mathrm{res}}}

is the BV Laplacian on
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{small}}
.

Finally, the result of the full integration over residual fields (
34
) satisfies the BFV cocycle (gauge-invariance) condition

\left(\Omega^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}\right)\;Z_{*}=0.

5.
Chern–Simons theory in “parallel ghost polarization”

In three-dimensional Chern–Simons theory there is another way of picking a pair of polarizations on the opposite sides of a cylinder: we can use the
(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{out}},\mathsf{A}^{0,1}_{\mathrm{out}})
representation on the out-boundary surface and the
(\mathsf{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathsf{A}^{1,0}_{\mathrm{in}})
representation on the in-surface. Thus, the corresponding polarizations are transversal in ghost number 0 and parallel in ghost number
\neq 0
. See also the discussion of quantization of 1D systems with this class of polarizations in
[
16
, Section 11]
.

5.1.
One-dimensional Chern–Simons theory with values in a cochain complex

As a warm-up, we consider again the one-dimensional theory, with a slightly different setup. Fix an odd integer
k
. Let

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus\mathfrak{g}^{i}

be a graded vector space with a differential
d_{\mathfrak{g}}
and a compatible graded symmetric pairing
(\cdot,\cdot)
of degree
-2k
.
29
29

29

The prime example being
\mathfrak{g}=\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
with
M
a
2k
-manifold,
d_{\mathfrak{g}}
the de Rham differential and
(\alpha,\beta)=\int_{M}\alpha\wedge\beta
.
Now, we let
X=\mathfrak{g}[k]
- this is a 0-shifted graded symplectic vector space. We call the induced grading on
C^{\infty}(X)
the ghost number. It is convenient to express elements of
C^{\infty}(X)
in terms of the shifted identity map
\psi\in\mathrm{Hom}(X,\mathfrak{g})
which has total degree (ghost number + degree)
k
.
30
30

30

If
\{t_{a}\}
is a basis in
\mathfrak{g}
and
\{\psi^{a}\}
is the shifted dual basis in
X^{*}
, then we have
\psi=\sum_{a}t_{a}\psi^{a}\in\mathrm{Hom}(X,\mathfrak{g})
. If the degree of
t_{a}
is
|a|
, then the ghost number of
\psi^{a}
is
k-|a|
, and thus
\psi
is indeed an object of total degree
k
.

We denote the ghost number
l
component of a field
\varphi
by
\varphi^{[l]}
. In particular, the ghost number
l
component
\psi^{[l]}
of
\psi
has degree
k-l
. For instance, the function

\Theta(\psi)=\frac{1}{2}(\psi,d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi)

has ghost number
+1
. Its hamiltonian vector field
Q
has ghost number
+1
and satisfies
Q^{2}=0
, thus
(X,\omega,Q)
is a BFV vector space.

We split the complexification of the ghost number
0
component of
X
as

X^{[0]}_{\mathbb{C}}=X^{+}\oplus X^{-}
, with
X^{\pm}
the degree 0
\pm i
-eigenspaces of a complex structure
J
on
X^{[0]}
compatible with the pairing. Thus,
X_{\mathbb{C}}
admits the total decomposition

(42)

X_{\mathbb{C}}=X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus X^{+}\oplus X^{-}\oplus X^{[<0]}_{\mathbb{C}}

where
X^{[>0]},X^{[<0]}
are the components of positive (resp. negative) ghost number.
31
31

31

I.e., coordinates, e.g., on
X^{[>0]}
have positive ghost number so
X^{[>0]}=\bigoplus_{i<k}\mathfrak{g}^{i}[k]
.
We also introduce the notations
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}
for the composition of the differential
X^{[1]}\to X^{+}\oplus X^{-}
with projections and similarly for the restriction of the differential
{X^{-}\oplus X^{+}\to X^{[-1]}}
(so that
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}=d_{\mathfrak{g}}\big|_{X^{+}}
,
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}=d_{\mathfrak{g}}\big|_{X^{-}}
). We automatically have

(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+})^{2}=(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-})^{2}=0
and
{d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}=-d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}}
.
32
32

32

A particularly important example is the case
\mathfrak{g}=\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
where
M
is a
2k
-manifold, with
k=2l+1
odd, endowed with a complex structure that allows us to decompose
\Omega^{k}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)=\Omega^{+}(M)\oplus\Omega^{-}(M)
into lagrangian subspaces with respect to
(\alpha,\beta)=\int_{M}\alpha\wedge\beta
, with the splitting given by (
106
) below.
In the case
k=1
, we have
d^{+}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\partial,d^{-}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\bar{\partial}
.

5.1.1.
Setup

We now consider the 1-dimensional AKSZ theory with target the symplectic graded vector space
(X,(\cdot,\cdot))
and hamiltonian
\Theta(\psi)
.
The space of fields is

\mathcal{F}=\Omega^{\bullet}(I;X).

It is parametrized by the superfield
\mathcal{A}
valued in
\Omega^{\bullet}(I;\mathfrak{g})
. We denote the 0- and 1-form components of
\mathcal{A}
by
\psi
and
A
, respectively.
The total degrees of
\mathcal{A},\psi,A
are all odd.
The action is

S[\psi+A]=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}({\mathcal{A}},d_{I}\mathcal{A})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\mathcal{A},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\psi,d_{I}\psi)+\int_{I}(A,d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi).

The space of boundary fields is

\mathcal{F}^{\partial}=X_{\mathrm{in}}\times X_{\mathrm{out}}\ni(\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}).

The boundary 1-form is

\alpha_{\partial I}=\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{out}}+\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{in}}=\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}})=\frac{1}{2}(\psi,\delta\psi)\bigg|_{t=0}^{t=1}.

Splitting elements of
X_{\mathbb{C}}

according to (
42
),

\psi=\psi^{[<0]}+\psi^{+}+\psi^{-}+\psi^{[>0]}
,
the boundary 1-form splits similarly:

\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{out}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big[(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[<0]},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[>0]})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[>0]},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[<0]})\Big]

and similarly for
\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{in}}
.

5.1.2.
Parallel ghost polarization

Let us now consider the case where the polarizations are parallel in the ghost sector (of the target) and transversal in the physical sector:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-}}\right\},

so that we are using the
(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}})
representation at
t=0
and the
(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}})
representation at
t=1
, i.e.:

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{in}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{out}},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{in}}=X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus X^{-},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{out}}=X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus X^{+}.

The polarized 1-form is

\alpha^{f}_{\partial I}=\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}}
with

\displaystyle\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}}

\displaystyle=-(\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\delta\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}})-(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-})=\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{in}}-\delta f_{\mathrm{in}},

\displaystyle\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle=(\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\delta\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+})=\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{out}}+\delta f_{\mathrm{out}},

where
33
33

33

Since
\psi
is odd, we have, e.g.,
\delta(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})=(\psi^{-},\delta\psi^{+})-(\psi^{+},\delta\psi^{-})
.

f_{\mathrm{in}}=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}})
,
f_{\mathrm{out}}=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}})+\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-})
,
so that

\alpha^{f}_{\partial I}=\alpha_{\partial I}+\delta f

with

f(\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{in}})=f_{\mathrm{out}}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}})-f_{\mathrm{in}}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}})\\
=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}})+\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}})+\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}).

The polarized action is

S^{f}[\mathcal{A}]=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}({\mathcal{A}},d_{I}\mathcal{A})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\mathcal{A},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A})+f(\mathcal{A}).

5.1.3.
Gauge fixing

The kernel
\mathcal{Y}
of the map
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{B}
is

(43)

\mathcal{Y}=\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\partial I;X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}})\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{0\};X^{-})\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{1\};X^{+})\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I;X^{[<0]}_{\mathbb{C}}).

Choosing the minimal space of residual fields

\mathcal{V}=\langle dt\rangle\otimes X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\langle 1\rangle\otimes X^{[<0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\ni{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{res}}=dt\cdot A_{\mathrm{res}}+1\cdot\psi_{\mathrm{res}},

we obtain

\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\ni(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{res}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{fl}})

with

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}=\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\partial I;X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}})_{\int=0}\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{0\};X^{-})\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I,\{1\};X^{+})\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(I;X^{[<0]}_{\mathbb{C}})_{\int\cdot\wedge dt=0}.

Here the notation
\int\cdot=0
(resp.
\int\cdot dt=0
) denotes acylic subcomplexes of forms with vanishing integral (resp. forms whose product with
dt
has vanishing integral).
Choosing an extension

{\widetilde{\psi}}=\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\widetilde{\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}}+\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\widetilde{\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}}

of boundary fields into the bulk, we obtain a splitting of

\mathcal{A}=\psi+A

into

\psi+A=\widetilde{\psi}+\psi_{\mathrm{res}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}+dt\cdot A_{\mathrm{res}}+A_{\mathrm{fl}}.

Inside
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
, we have the gauge-fixing lagrangian
\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
given by forms of degree 0 in
I
— i.e.,
\mathcal{L}
is given by
A_{\mathrm{fl}}=0
— and we write for
\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}\in\mathcal{L}

\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}=\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[>0]}+\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]}.

Recollecting, for a field
\psi+A\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{L}
we obtain

(44)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\psi}+\psi_{\mathrm{res}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\widetilde{\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}}+\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\widetilde{\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}}+\psi_{\mathrm{res}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[>0]}+\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]},

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=dt\cdot A_{\mathrm{res}}.

The gauge-fixed polarized action is then computed as follows:

Lemma 5.1
.

Restricted to the gauge-fixing lagrangian, the polarized action can be written as

(45)

S^{f}[\mathsf{A}]=S_{\mathrm{source}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}]+S_{0}[\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}]+S_{\mathrm{int}}[\psi_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]+S_{\mathrm{back}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},A_{\mathrm{res}}],

where

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{source}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}]

\displaystyle=(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-}(1))-(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+}(0))+(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1))-(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]}(0)),

\displaystyle S_{0}[\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}]

\displaystyle=\int_{I}(\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+},d_{I}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-})+\int_{I}(\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{fl}},d_{I}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[>0]}),

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{int}}[\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]

\displaystyle=-\int_{I}\,dt(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-})-\int_{I}\,dt(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}})+\int_{I}dt(A_{\mathrm{res}}^{[>1]},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]}),

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{back}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]

\displaystyle=(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}})+(A_{\mathrm{res}}^{[>1]},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}}).

Here we have introduced the notation
A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},A^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
for the components of
A_{\mathrm{res}}

valued in
X^{[1]}
and in
X^{[>1]}
, respectively.
34
34

34

We understand
A^{[k]}_{\mathrm{res}}
as valued in
X^{[k]}
but with ghost number
k-1
; the shift is due to the fact that
A^{[k]}_{\mathrm{res}}
is a coefficient of a
1
-form on the source. This shift is a standard feature of the AKSZ construction. In particular,
A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
is an object of ghost number
zero
.

Proof.

The polarized action is

S^{f}[\psi+A]=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\psi,d_{I}\psi)+\int_{I}(A,d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi)+f(\psi),

where

\displaystyle f(\psi)

\displaystyle=f_{\mathrm{out}}(\psi(1))-f_{\mathrm{in}}(\psi(0))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1))+\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1))-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{fl}}(0))+\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}(0),\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}).

Splitting
\psi
as in (
44
) and letting the support of
\widetilde{\psi}
go towards
\partial I
we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\psi,d_{I}\psi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\widetilde{\psi}+\psi_{\mathrm{res}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},d_{I}(\widetilde{\psi}+\psi_{\mathrm{res}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\psi},\psi_{\mathrm{res}})\bigg|^{t=1}_{t=0}+\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\psi},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}})\bigg|^{t=1}_{t=0}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}(\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},d_{I}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}})+\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]}(1))+\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-}(1))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{fl}}(0))-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+}(0))+\int_{I}(\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{+},d_{I}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-})+\int_{I}(\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]},d_{I}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[>0]})

and

\displaystyle\int_{I}(dt\cdot A_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi)=

\displaystyle-\int_{I}\,dt(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{-})-\int_{I}\,dt(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}})

\displaystyle+\int_{I}dt(A_{\mathrm{res}}^{[>1]},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]})+(A_{\mathrm{res}}^{[>1]},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}}).

Collecting the various terms, we obtain (
45
). ∎

Notice that adding
f
has the effect of doubling the boundary source terms.

5.1.4.
Effective action

The effective action is defined by

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S_{\mathrm{eff}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]}=\int\mathcal{D}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}\,e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S^{f}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S_{\mathrm{back}}}\int\mathcal{D}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}\,e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}(S_{\mathrm{source}}+S_{0}+S_{\mathrm{int}})}

where the integral is defined in terms of Feynman diagrams.

Proposition 5.2
.

The effective action is given by

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{eff}}

\displaystyle=S_{\mathrm{ph}}+S_{\mathrm{gh}}\quad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{ph}}

\displaystyle=(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}})+(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}),

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{gh}}

\displaystyle=(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}})+(A^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}}).

Proof.

In terms of Feynman diagrams,
S_{\mathrm{source}}
generates boundary vertices (figure
2(a)
),
S_{\mathrm{int}}
generates bulk vertices (Figure
2(b)
).
35
35

35

We are ignoring here the ghost vertices that in this case do not contribute to the effective action since no vertex carries as
\psi^{[>0]}
.

\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}
\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}

(a)

Boundary vertices

d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}

(b)

Bulk vertices

Figure 2
.
Vertices in 1D AKSZ theories with linear polarizations

The term
S_{0}
generates the propagators

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{ph}}(t,t^{\prime})=\frac{i}{\hbar}\langle\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}(t)\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(t^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=(\theta(t^{\prime}-t))\cdot\omega^{-1}

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{gh}}(t,t^{\prime})=\frac{i}{\hbar}\langle\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[>0]}(t)\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[<0]}(t^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=(\theta(t-t^{\prime})-t)\cdot\omega^{-1}

with
\theta(t)
the Heaviside function and
\omega^{-1}
the inverse of the pairing on
\mathfrak{g}
. There are three types of connected Feynman diagrams contributing to
S_{\mathrm{eff}}
(see Figure
3
):

(1)

A single edge connecting the two boundary vertices (Figure
3(a)
). That diagram evaluates to
(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+})
.

(2)

A single edge connecting a boundary vertex to a bulk vertex (Figure
3(b)
)
. Those diagrams yield
(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}).

(3)

A single edge connecting the two bulk vertices (Figure
3(c)
)
. This diagram gives, using
\int_{I\times I}\eta_{\mathrm{ph}}(t,t^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}
, the contribution
\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})
.

\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}
\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}

(a)

Single edge connecting two boundaries

\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}
\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}

(b)

Single edge connecting bulk and boundary

d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}

(c)

Single edge connecting two bulk vertices

Figure 3
.
Connected Feynman diagrams in effective action

In total we obtain the effective action

(46)

S_{\mathrm{eff}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]=(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}})-(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}})\\
+(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+(A^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}}).

Separating the term depending only on ghost number 0 fields from the rest, we obtain the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

The lagrangian generated by the
\mathrm{gh}=0
part of the action is the evolution relation in
X^{[0]}_{\mathrm{in}}\times X^{[0]}_{\mathrm{out}}
.

Proof.

The Euler-Lagrange equations of the theory in ghost number 0 are

\displaystyle d_{I}\psi^{[0]}

\displaystyle=-d_{\mathfrak{g}}A^{[1]},

\displaystyle d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{[0]}

\displaystyle=0.

Projecting to boundary values
(\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}})
we obtain
the equations

(47)

\displaystyle d^{-}_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=d^{+}_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{out}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}=0,

(48)

\displaystyle\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=d^{+}_{\mathfrak{g}}a,

(49)

\displaystyle\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=d^{-}_{\mathfrak{g}}b,

for some
a,b\in X^{[1]}
, and the first equation forces
a=b
(up to a
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
and
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}
-closed term).
On the other hand, the lagrangian generated by
S_{\mathrm{ph}}
is given by

\displaystyle\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial S_{\mathrm{ph}}}{\partial\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}}=\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}-d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},

\displaystyle\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial S_{\mathrm{ph}}}{\partial\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}}=\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial S_{\mathrm{ph}}}{\partial A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}}=d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}.

The first two equations are equivalent to equations (
48
),(
49
), while the last equation enforces the constraint (
47
).
∎

5.1.5.
Quantum master equation

The modified quantum master equation

(-\hbar^{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}+\Omega)e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S_{\mathrm{eff}}}

is equivalent to

(50)

\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\{S_{\mathrm{eff}},S_{\mathrm{eff}}\right\}_{\mathrm{res}}-i\hbar\Delta_{\mathrm{res}}S_{\mathrm{eff}}+\Omega\right)Z=0.

Here we denote by
\{\cdot,\cdot\}_{\mathrm{res}}
the BV (
+1
-shifted Poisson) bracket on
\mathcal{V}
.

Proposition 5.4
.

The effective action
S_{\mathrm{eff}}
given by (
46
) satisfies the mQME (
50
) with boundary BFV operator
\Omega
given by the standard quantization of

\Theta(\psi)=\frac{1}{2}(\psi,d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi)=(\psi^{+},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{[1]})+(\psi^{-},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{[1]})+(\psi^{[<0]},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{[>1]}).

Proof.

Expanding degree-wise as a differential operator, we obtain
\Omega=\Omega^{(1)}+\Omega^{(0)}=\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{(0)}_{\mathrm{out}}+\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{in}}+\Omega^{(0)}_{\mathrm{in}}
with

\displaystyle\Omega^{(0)}_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}\right),

\displaystyle\Omega^{(0)}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=-\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{out}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+}}\right)+\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[>1]},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{[>0]}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}}\right)+\left(d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}}}\right).

First of all, notice that
\Delta_{\mathcal{V}}S_{\mathrm{eff}}=0
since in the only possibly nonvanishing term
(A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}})
fields are not paired with their antifields because of the degree shift by the differential. Computing the BV bracket we obtain

(51)

\frac{1}{2}\{S_{\mathrm{eff}},S_{\mathrm{eff}}\}_{\mathrm{res}}=\\
=-(\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})-(\psi^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{out}}-\psi^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi_{\mathrm{res}})

(only terms of opposite ghost number survive in the pairing).
On the other hand, since
\Omega^{(0)}
is a multiplication operator and
\Omega^{(1)}
contains only derivatives of first order, we have
\Omega Z=\Omega^{(0)}Z+\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}(S_{\mathrm{eff}})Z
and

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{out}}S_{\mathrm{eff}}

\displaystyle=(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{out}})+(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{out}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})+(d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}}),

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}_{\mathrm{in}}S_{\mathrm{eff}}

\displaystyle=-(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{out}})+(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}\psi^{[1]}_{\mathrm{in}},d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})-(d_{\mathfrak{g}}\psi^{[>1]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}}).

A straightforward computation shows that
\Omega^{(0)}+\frac{i}{\hbar}\Omega^{(1)}S_{\mathrm{eff}}
coincides with (
51
), thus completing the proof. ∎

5.2.
General polarizations

Next, we will consider the case where
X^{[0]}_{\mathbb{C}}
is equipped with another polarization
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{nl}}
which is not necessarily linear (see
[
16
, Section 12]
for the corresponding toy model). Let the base
\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{nl}}}
be locally parametrized by a coordinate
\psi^{Q}
, and the fibers by a coordinate
\psi^{P}
. Suppose
G(\psi^{-},\psi^{Q})
is a generating function of the canonical
transformation
36
36

36

I.e., the function
G(\psi^{-},\psi^{Q})
satisfying
\delta G=\psi^{+}\delta\psi^{-}-\psi^{P}\delta\psi^{Q}
.

(\psi^{-},\psi^{+})\to(\psi^{Q},\psi^{P})
. Then we have that
\psi^{+}=F(\psi^{-},\psi^{Q})=\frac{\partial G}{\partial\psi^{-}}
. We assume that
G
is analytic in
\psi^{-}
in a neighborhood
U
of
\{0\}\times\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{nl}}}
.

We now consider again the 1D AKSZ theory on the interval, where we choose the polarizations at the two endpoints to be parallel in the ghost sector. In the physical sector we choose the
\psi^{-}
-representation on the in-boundary and the
\psi^{Q}
-representation on the out-boundary:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}\quad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}}},\frac{\delta}{\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{P}}\right\}.

The base is

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{in}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{out}},\;\;\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{in}}=X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus X^{-},\;\;\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{out}}=X^{[>0]}_{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{nl}}}.

The polarized 1-form is

\alpha^{f}_{\partial I}=\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}}
with

\displaystyle\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{in}}}

\displaystyle=-(\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\delta\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}})-(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-})=\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{in}}-\delta f_{\mathrm{in}},

\displaystyle\alpha^{\partial,\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle=(\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\delta\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}})+(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{P},\delta\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q})=\alpha^{\partial}_{\mathrm{out}}+\delta f_{\mathrm{out}},

where

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}),

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-})-G(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}}).

Thus,

\alpha^{f}_{\partial I}=\alpha_{\partial I}+\delta f

with

f(\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{in}})=f_{\mathrm{out}}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}})-f_{\mathrm{in}}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}})\\
=\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{out}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{-})-G(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}})-\frac{1}{2}(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[<0]}_{\mathrm{in}})+\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{+},\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}).

5.2.1.
Splitting the fields

The goal is to find again a symplectomorphism
\Phi\colon\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}
.
37
37

37

Strictly speaking, the range of
\Phi
is not
\mathcal{F}
, but rather a certain regularization of
\mathcal{F}
more suitable for quantization. See the discussion in
[
16
, Section 9.2.3]
.
Here the trick is that we keep the space of fluctuations
\mathcal{Y}
as above in Equation (
43
). In ghost number zero, the map
\Phi
is defined as follows. For boundary values
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{in}}^{[0]}
and
\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{out}}^{[0]}
and fluctuations
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}\in\mathcal{Y}
(recall that
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(0)=\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1)=0
), we let

\psi^{-}(t)=\begin{cases}\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(t)&\mathrm{for}\;\;t>0,\\
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}&\mathrm{for}\;\;t=0,\end{cases}

and

\psi^{+}(t)=\begin{cases}\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}(t)&\mathrm{for}\;\;t<1,\\
F(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1),\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}})&\mathrm{for}\;\;t=1.\end{cases}

The map
\Phi
is given by

(52)

\Phi(\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},A)=\psi^{-}(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}})+\psi^{+}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}},\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}})\\
+{\psi_{\mathrm{res}}}+\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{in}}}+\widetilde{\psi^{[>0]}_{\mathrm{out}}}+\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}^{[\neq 0]}+A.

In nonzero ghost number, this coincides with the splitting considered in the previous section. In what follows, we will discuss only the physical sector, i.e., the part in ghost number 0. The analysis in the ghost sector proceeds exactly as in Section
5.1
and results in the ghost effective action
S_{\mathrm{gh}}
described in Proposition
5.2
.

5.2.2.
Effective action

Again, we can use the gauge-fixing lagrangian
\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
given by zero forms. Restricted to
\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{L}
and fields of ghost number 0, we have

S^{f}[\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}},dt\cdot A_{\mathrm{res}}]=-(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}(0))-G(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}}(1),\psi^{Q}_{\mathrm{out}})\\
+\int_{I}(\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}},d_{I}\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}})-dt(d^{+}_{\mathfrak{g}}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{fl}})-dt(d^{-}_{\mathfrak{g}}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}})

where the computation is very similar to the one in the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
The BV-BFV effective action is defined by

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S_{\mathrm{eff}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]}=\int\mathcal{D}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S^{f}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{fl}},A_{\mathrm{res}}]}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}S_{\mathrm{back}}}\int\mathcal{D}\psi_{\mathrm{fl}}\;e^{\frac{i}{\hbar}(S_{\mathrm{source}}+S_{0}+S_{\mathrm{int}})}

where the integral is defined in terms of Feynman diagrams.

Proposition 5.5
.

The effective action (in ghost number 0) is

(53)

S_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{ph}}[\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q},A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}]=-G(\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q})-(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}+\frac{1}{2}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}})

Proof.

In terms of Feynman diagrams, the source term creates a vertex of arbitrary incoming valence on the out-boundary decorated by derivatives of
G
, and a univalent (outgoing) vertex on the in-boundary decorated by
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}
.
The interaction term creates univalent in- and outgoing bulk vertices decorated by
d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\pm}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
as in the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q}
i_{1}
i_{2}
i_{k}
\leadsto\frac{\partial G(x,y)}{\partial x^{i_{1}}\cdots\partial x^{i_{k}}}\big|_{(x,y)=(0,\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q})}

Figure 4
.
Additional vertex in 1D AKSZ theory with a general polarization on
X_{\mathrm{out}}^{[0]}
.

The connected Feynman diagrams contributing to the effective action are:

(1)

Diagrams involving the
G
-vertex on the out-boundary. The outgoing half-edges can connect either to the bulk vertex involving
\psi^{+}_{\mathrm{fl}}
or the vertex on the in-boundary (see Figure
5
). Summing over all valences, we obtain the Taylor series in
x
of
G(x,y)
in the first argument at
(0,\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q})

evaluated on
\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}
. Hence, by analyticity of
G

those vertices sum up to

-G(\psi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-}+d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Q}).

(2)

Diagrams involving the univalent incoming bulk vertex. Here the outgoing half-edges connect to either the vertex on the in-boundary or
an outgoing bulk vertex,
giving

-(d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}},\psi^{-}_{\mathrm{in}}+\frac{1}{2}d_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-}A^{[1]}_{\mathrm{res}}).

Those diagrams are the same as in the linear case (Figure
3
).

\gamma_{\mathrm{in}}
